Quoted line from the reply: "Detect with a query, not a flag. Flag a payment with `status = Captured` and no `ledger_entries` row of type `Hold` for its lesson. Don't rely on anything the catch block writes, because those writes can fail for the same reason the original write did."

Full advice (paraphrased except the quoted line above):
1. **Housekeeping first**: flagged the `086da90` bundling mistake above (corrected, see this window's NOTE) and two smaller R75/format issues in the just-written START entry (also corrected/disclosed above).
2. **Item 3 mechanism**: detect stranded captures by query (Captured payment + no Hold-type ledger entry for its lesson), not by a new column — this needs no schema change and no v1.4 deviation note, so the "nullable column/enum" allowance in R77 goes unused unless a later item needs it. Close the mirror case (finding #1's "Payment::create fails after a successful capture") by writing the Payment row as `Pending` *before* calling the gateway (existing enum value, no schema change) and updating it to `Captured`/`Failed` after — with a second detection clause for a `Pending` row older than a short grace period, which also covers finding #15 (an unknown-outcome gateway exception). Flagged a precondition to check first: whether `BookLesson`'s caller wraps it in an outer `DB::transaction` — if so, the pre-capture `Pending` write is not durable and the design does not hold; verifying this before writing code.
3. Catch `Throwable` (not just `LessonTransitionException`) around everything from after `capture()` returns to the end of the method; on catch, attempt `transition(Expired)` in its own nested try/catch so a failure there does not mask the original error; rethrow `BookingException` wrapping the *original* throwable; no refund attempt (R77 constraint, fake gateway can't refund anyway).
4. `ledger:verify` extended to fail with one line per flagged payment (id + lesson id), with a short creation-time grace window so an in-flight request is never flagged mid-booking.
5. Test-writing traps for this money code: `LedgerService` is `final` (no subclassing/plain Mockery — use an `LedgerEntry::creating` listener or `Payment::updating` listener to inject a failure, or `app()->instance()` + `forgetInstance()` before running `ledger:verify` since its `handle()` type-hints the real class); throw a plain `RuntimeException` in test doubles, never a real SQL error (Postgres aborts the whole `RefreshDatabase` transaction on an unhandled SQL error, breaking every assertion after it); extend the existing "sweep won" test (`BookLessonTest.php:325-360`, criticised by round-1 finding #1 for "documenting the state rather than preventing it") to also assert `ledger:verify` fails and names that lesson.

Adopting this design for item 3: query-based detection (no schema change, no migration edit needed — sidesteps the forward-only-migration question entirely), pre-capture `Pending` Payment row for the mirror case, broad `Throwable` catch with an Expired-transition attempt and original-exception preservation, `ledger:verify` extended with a grace window, and the listed test-injection technique. Checking `BookLesson`'s caller for an outer transaction next, before writing code.

PHPStan caught a real typing gap while wiring `strandedPayments()`'s return: mapping straight to `'status' => $payment->status->value` typed the array shape's `status` as the enum's literal backing-value union (`'captured'|'failed'|...`), not the declared `string` — and `Collection`'s `TValue` is invariant, so the narrower-typed collection didn't satisfy the wider-typed return signature even though `string` was the semantically-correct, intentional declaration (callers — `VerifyLedger`, the test) only care that it's a string, never which literal). A `(string)` cast on the value did not widen it (PHPStan treats an explicit cast as a no-op when the source is already string-typed, literal or not). Fixed properly, not suppressed: extracted a private `statusValue(Payment $payment): string` helper — an explicit function return-type declaration is a trusted boundary PHPStan widens through, unlike a cast — and mapped through that instead. `vendor/bin/phpstan analyse app/Services/Ledger/LedgerService.php` confirms `{"tool":"phpstan","result":"passed","errors":0}` after the fix.

Two tests, per R77's literal acceptance text ("the stranded case is detected by ledger:verify; a normal booking is unaffected"): (1) extended the existing "sweep won" race test (`BookLessonTest.php`, `'records a captured payment but no HOLD when the sweep expires the lesson before confirmation'`) with `$this->travel(6)->minutes()` past the grace window followed by `$this->artisan('ledger:verify')->expectsOutputToContain(...)->assertExitCode(1)` — traced the new `BookLesson` code by hand against this exact scenario (fake gateway transitions the lesson to `Expired` mid-`capture()`) first and confirmed the original three assertions (lesson `Expired`, payment `Captured`, zero ledger entries) are unaffected by the new pre-capture-`Pending`/broad-catch code, so no regression; (2) added a new test, `'leaves ledger:verify clean after a normal successful booking, even past the stranded-payment grace window'` — an ordinary booking, `$this->travel(6)->minutes()`, then asserts `ledger:verify` still prints "Ledger OK" and exits 0, closing the false-positive risk the grace window exists to prevent.

[2026-09-24 04:52] NOTE — R77 item 4 implemented and green. `app/Console/Commands/ExpireUnpaidLessons.php`: the query chain gained `->lazyById()` immediately before `->each()` (was: `->each()` directly on the builder); the `LessonStateMachine::transition($lesson, LessonStatus::Expired)` call gained a third argument, a `$work` closure run on the just-locked model that throws `LessonTransitionException` when `$locked->payment?->status === PaymentStatus::Captured`, falling into the same pre-existing `catch (LessonTransitionException)` block the "moved on" case already used — no new exception type, no change to the catch structure. Rationale for reusing that exception rather than adding a dedicated one: both cases mean the same thing to the caller ("this lesson moved on between the query and the lock — nothing left to expire"), and the state machine's own `assert()` already throws the identical type when a *committed* status change (e.g. already `confirmed`) makes the edge illegal outright; this closure only covers the narrower window where the payment is `captured` but the status column itself is still `pending_payment` in the DB (BookLesson's own capture-then-confirm gap), which `assert()` cannot see.

One test, matching the pagination-change scope note in R77 item 4's own text ("the pagination change needs no new test beyond the existing count assertion"): `'skips a lesson whose payment captures between the sweep's query and its row lock'`, added to `tests/Feature/Lessons/ExpireUnpaidLessonsTest.php`. Technique: a `DB::listen()` hook armed before calling the command, firing exactly once on the sweep's own outer, un-locked listing query (matched by SQL containing `"lessons"` and not `for update`) — captures the payment right there, simulating `BookLesson` winning the race in the gap `lazyById()`'s paging opens up. First attempt fired the hook on the *lock* query itself (`for update`) instead; that failed the payment-stays-captured assertion, because the update landed inside `LessonStateMachine::transition()`'s own `DB::transaction()`, and the `$work` closure's `LessonTransitionException` (thrown deliberately, to skip the edge) rolled that whole transaction back — including the injected update, reverting the payment to `pending` before the assertion ran. Caught immediately by the test's own failure output ("Expected Captured, Actual Pending"), not by inspection; corrected by moving the injection to the un-locked outer query instead, which sits outside any transaction the sweep opens and so survives the later rollback. `ExpireUnpaidLessonsTest.php` green: 6 passed (up from 5), 15 assertions.

`composer test` itself could not finish locally. Composer stopped it at its 300-second process timeout because the tests run slower on this machine, so I ran each step on its own.

To get the worktree running, I made three local changes, all git-ignored: stopped a `composer install` after 10 minutes (per CLAUDE.md's stall rule) and copied `vendor/` from the main checkout (composer.lock files identical); copied `public/build` from the main checkout; created `.env` from `.env.example`.

**Verdicts**

1. Round-1 #1 (Medium, stranded payment): PASS WITH NOTE. It is closed. Discount cap: `trial_discount_pct` capped at 99 (`app/Filament/Pages/ManageSettings.php:75`). Guard before charging: `BookLesson.php:98-100` rejects a price of zero or below before `LessonStateMachine::open` runs — no lesson, no payment row, no gateway call. Detection: the payment row is now written `pending` before the gateway is called, and every error after a successful charge is caught; `LedgerService::strandedPayments()` flags a captured payment with no hold, or a payment still pending, once more than 5 minutes old. Proved by a probe: made `hold()` throw a plain `RuntimeException` after the charge — result was a `BookingException`, lesson ended `expired`, payment stayed `captured`, no ledger rows; `ledger:verify` clean inside the grace window, then after 6 minutes reported "Payment N on lesson M is captured with no matching ledger hold" and exited 1. Note: on this path `BookLesson` itself expires a lesson whose money was already taken — that frees the slot, and `expired` has no onward state, so there is no refund route; also `ledger:verify` is not scheduled in `routes/console.php`, so "detected" means detected when someone runs it. With the new guard, this path can only be reached through database-level failures.

2. NEW: FAIL(Low, `tests/Feature/Lessons/BookLessonTest.php:509-553`, test gap). No committed test covers the widening to catch every error. Changed `catch (Throwable $e)` at `BookLesson.php:163` back to `catch (LessonTransitionException $e)` and ran `tests/Feature/Lessons` and `tests/Feature/Ledger` — all 421 committed tests still passed, only the probe failed. The item-3 test reaches the catch through a `LessonTransitionException`, which round 1 already caught. The fix is a test where `hold()` throws some other exception (the probe did this by swapping `LedgerService` in the container with a stand-in whose `hold()` throws).

3. Round-1 #2 (sweep re-check after the lock): PASS WITH NOTE. The re-check exists at `ExpireUnpaidLessons.php:56-60`, runs after `lockForUpdate`, loads the payment fresh inside the transaction. Mutation check: disabling the re-check failed the R77 item 4 test ("Expected PendingPayment, got Expired"), confirming the test catches a regression. Remaining race: the switch to `captured` (`BookLesson.php:157`) still happens outside the lesson lock — can only matter if a charge takes more than 15 minutes, and `ledger:verify` would flag it.

4. Round-1 #3 (`->each()` skipping rows): PASS. `lazyById()` pages by id, so rows leaving the filtered set mid-run cannot cause skips.

5. Round-1 #4 (float/round grep): PASS WITH NOTE. `LedgerServiceTest.php:272-293` covers every file named. The Payments folder is covered by three hard-coded file paths rather than a wildcard, so new files added there at CP5 will not be scanned automatically. `Onboarding.vue:122` has `(fils / 100).toFixed(2)`, a display-only float, present before this PR — the pattern does not match it.

6. Round-1 #5 (preview and booked price agree across the band table): PASS. Both data tables come from `LevelTier::band()`, the same source `PriceBandSeeder` uses; cover all three tiers at minimum, maximum and an odd-fils rate — real seeded values, not synthetic. The one-tutor test (`BookLessonTest.php:274`) compares the page's `trialPriceFils` value directly with the booked lesson's price, with no `trialPrice()` call in between.

7. Round-1 #7 (MoneyTest comment): PASS. The comment now correctly says the rejected approach "would give 76".

8. NEW, PASS WITH NOTE (latent; nothing calls these paths yet): if an after-commit callback ever throws once the lesson is confirmed (e.g. a queued listener with Redis down), `BookLesson` would report "could no longer be confirmed" for a lesson that is confirmed and paid. `BookingException` now covers both rejected input and "customer charged, needs manual review" — a future controller that turns it into a friendly message would stop that case from ever being logged. `VerifyLedger` only checks that `ledger_entries` exists — on rehearsal, between a deploy and the separately approved `migrate`, it would crash on the missing `payments` table instead of failing cleanly. A gateway error other than `PaymentCaptureException` leaves the payment `pending` — `ledger:verify` would then fail until someone fixes that row by hand, correct for an unknown outcome but with no reconciliation path yet.

I deleted the probe and reverted both mutations; `git status` is clean. I made no commits."

[2026-09-24 14:35] ADVISOR — Design consult for `CancelLesson`/`SkipLesson` (rule 11's mandatory money/ledger trigger), before any code written. Model: not reported by the tool; configured advisor per PROJECT_BRIEF is Fable 5.1 — configured, not measured.

Put to the advisor: the full state machine edge table, `LedgerService`'s hold/release/refund API and preconditions, PRD §4's cancellation table, the existing `tutor_strikes`/`SuspendTutor`/`TutorStatusTransitions`/admin-email (`AdminRecipients`, `SendTutorPermitExpiredMail`) patterns, and a draft design: two-step parent-cancel transition (confirmed -> cancelled_by_parent -> {refunded|completed_reported}), one-step tutor-cancel/skip transition, `diffInHours` window math, strike count-and-suspend as plain sequential code after the lesson transition commits, widening `SuspendTutor` to a nullable admin, and a scope claim that reschedule/routes/parent-tutor cancel emails are all out of scope this sub-cycle (deferred to 3e).

Advisor's verdict, quoting the one line that mattered most: "the crash-between-commits shape and the strike race are the two things that would have come back as Medium findings." Corrections, all adopted:
(1) Wrap the parent path's two transitions in one outer `DB::transaction()` — two separately-committed transitions leave `cancelled_by_parent` stranded (full price in escrow, no sweep, not a terminal state) if the process dies between them; nesting is already proven (`LedgerService::operate()` already nests inside `LessonStateMachine::transition()` in `BookLesson.php`).
(2) Window math: Carbon 3 (Laravel 13) makes `diffInHours()` return a float with `$absolute` defaulting false — replace with an instant comparison, `$deadline = $lesson->starts_at->copy()->subHours($lesson->cancel_window_hours); $insideWindow = now()->greaterThan($deadline)`, computed from the LOCKED row inside `$work` (not the caller's copy), preserving PRD's "≥24h" as refund-inclusive at the exact boundary.
(3) One frozen `cancel_window_hours` column is correct for BOTH the parent refund boundary and the tutor strike boundary — PRD/DATA_MODEL only ever describe one window; a second threshold would need a new setting/column/PRD change, none authorised.
(4) Strike-count-then-maybe-suspend as plain sequential code has a real race (two concurrent late tutor-cancels can both read count=3, both call `SuspendTutor`, the second throws `TutorStatusTransitionException` from inside an already-committed cancel, and both would dispatch the admin email). Fix: a new idempotent action `App\Actions\Tutor\SuspendTutorForStrikes`, locking the `TutorProfile` row inside its own `DB::transaction`, no-op if not `approved` or count < 3, dispatches `TutorSuspendedForStrikes` only on an actual suspension via `DB::afterCommit`.
(5) Widen `SuspendTutor::__invoke(?User $admin, ...)` — `RecordAuditLog` already tolerates a null actor for system-originated rows; keep `SuspendTutor` itself sending no email (unchanged for the manual Filament path), the admin email belongs to `SuspendTutorForStrikes` only.
(6) A gap I had not designed for at all: the `payments` row. `payments.lesson_id` is UNIQUE (R78) so refund updates the existing row, not a new one — set `status = Refunded`, `refunded_amount = price` inside the same `$work` closure as `LedgerService::refund()`; no gateway `refund()` call exists yet (R77/D-02, CP5) — logged as a `## Deferred` line in STATUS, not built here. RELEASE path leaves the payment `captured`.
(7) Set `escrow_released_at = now()` on the RELEASE path (parent-late-cancel) so CP5's balance buckets and CP4's report-due carry-forward treat it identically to a normal release.
(8) Refuse `CancelLesson`/`SkipLesson` once `now() >= starts_at` (a product-level default, simplest option keeping invariants intact, per CLAUDE.md's "When unsure" rule) — a confirmed lesson past its start is a no-show/join matter (CP4), not a cancellation.
(9) Actor check (tutor via `tutorProfile->user_id`, parent via `learner->account_user_id`, `withTrashed()` per R54) stays outside the lock; throw before touching the state machine.
(10) `SkipLesson`: cheap defensive assertion that escrow balance is 0 before proceeding (protects against a `reserved -> confirmed` race with CP4's charge job).
(11) Scope boundaries confirmed straight from PLAN.md/CHECKPOINTS.md text: no `RescheduleLesson` action, no routes/controllers, no parent/tutor cancelled-or-skipped emails (all 3e); `LedgerService` and `LessonStateMachine` need no edits.
(12) Full test list given (25h/23h/exact-boundary triple; settings-change-after-booking on both boundaries; `sum()===0` on every one of 7 paths; strike row shape; 3-strikes-suspends-with-audit-and-mail; 2-recent-plus-1-old does not suspend; a 3rd strike on an already-suspended tutor is a silent no-op; double-cancel throws `LessonTransitionException` with nothing new written; wrong-actor throws with the lesson unchanged; payment row state on both refund paths vs the release path).

Adopting all twelve points as the design for this sub-cycle's code. Advisor count for cycle 04 so far (CLAUDE.md-defined mechanism, not the built-in tool): 1.

[2026-09-24 14:41] DECISION — Design choices closing CP3 acceptance boxes 4–6, for the record: (1) one frozen `cancel_window_hours` column serves both the parent-refund boundary and the tutor-strike boundary — PRD/DATA_MODEL name only one window, so no second threshold was introduced. (2) The window boundary is refund-inclusive: cancelling AT the exact deadline instant refunds the parent (not tutor-paid); one second later, the tutor is paid. (3) `payments.refunded_amount`/`status` are updated on both parent-refund and tutor-cancel paths (the existing UNIQUE `lesson_id` row, per R78), left `captured` on the parent-late-cancel RELEASE path; no gateway `refund()` call exists yet — added to STATUS.md's `## Deferred` list (CP5/D-02). (4) `escrow_released_at` is set on the RELEASE path so CP5's balance buckets and CP4's report-due carry-forward treat it identically to a normal release. (5) `CancelLesson`/`SkipLesson` both refuse once `now() >= starts_at` — a confirmed/reserved lesson past its start is a no-show/join matter (CP4), not a cancellation; simplest option keeping invariants intact per CLAUDE.md's "When unsure" rule, no PRD text covers this case explicitly. (6) `SuspendTutorForStrikes` counts ALL `TutorStrike` rows (any `type`) within the trailing 90 days, not filtered to late-cancel only, matching PRD's generic "3 strikes" wording. (7) CHECKPOINTS.md CP3 acceptance boxes 4, 5 and 6 checked, each citing the specific test names, per the same citation style prior sub-cycles used.

A controller would report failure for an operation that actually succeeded, and a retry hits `LessonTransitionException` on the already-moved lesson. Called on a `reserved` lesson, it reaches `LedgerService::refund()`/`release()` and throws a generic `LedgerException` (escrow ≠ price) instead of a clear, well-named `CancellationException` like `SkipLesson`'s explicit `sum() !== 0` check. No corruption — the transaction rolls back cleanly — but the failure mode is unhelpful.

Summary from the agent: 13 PASS, 2 PASS WITH NOTE, 1 FAIL(Medium), 1 FAIL(Low). No frozen files or migrations touched.

The advisor flagged that Option 1's premise — "add a guard to `CancelLesson` mirroring `SkipLesson`'s pattern" — is false: `SkipLesson`'s only check (`sum($locked) !== 0`) is not a status guard, and per invariant #1 a lesson's ledger always sums to zero between operations, so that check can never fire against a `confirmed`, HOLD-ed lesson. Read `LedgerService::sum()` (app/Services/Ledger/LedgerService.php:127-130) directly to confirm: it is the lesson's full cross-account total, exactly the invariant-#1 quantity that is 0 after every legal operation, not an escrow-only balance. Cross-checked against `LessonStateMachine::edges()` (app/Services/Lessons/LessonStateMachine.php:52-55): `confirmed -> cancelled_by_tutor|cancelled_by_parent` is a legal edge, the same edge `reserved` uses, so the state machine itself does not stop `SkipLesson` from being invoked on a paid lesson. Net: calling `SkipLesson` on a `confirmed`, HOLD-ed lesson would have passed silently — no ledger write, no exception, lesson cancelled — permanently stranding the price in escrow (not refunded, not paid to the tutor). This is a Medium+ money defect the 15:10 review did not find (it only checked `CancelLesson`). Disclosing per rule 11 ("stale plan text → disclose ... never silently redo finished work, never silently do something else"): STATUS.md §7 Option 1's literal text ("mirroring SkipLesson's pattern") is corrected here rather than followed literally. Advisor's further guidance adopted: (1) add a real `status === Reserved` guard to `SkipLesson` and a real `status === Confirmed` guard to `CancelLesson`, both evaluated on the row locked inside `LessonStateMachine::transition()`'s `$work` closure — not a pre-lock check — since `recurring:charge` can move a lesson `reserved -> confirmed` concurrently with a skip attempt (TOCTOU); (2) the Medium fix (`Throwable` catch + `report()`, no rethrow) proceeds as Option 1 described, in both files; (3) write a regression test proving the `SkipLesson`-on-`confirmed` hole before fixing it, not just asserting the fix's shape. Judgment call: fixing both files' guards (not just `CancelLesson`'s, as Option 1's literal text asked) stays within this round's authorised scope — same PR, same two files already being touched, closing a more-precisely-diagnosed version of the same guard defect — rather than a scope-widening action requiring a fresh Owner action; proceeding on that basis per CLAUDE.md's "When unsure" rule (simplest option keeping invariants intact) and invariant #1's non-negotiable weight.

[2026-09-24 16:49] VERIFICATION — Sized the `ProfileController::destroy` defect the advisor flagged (app/Http/Controllers/Settings/ProfileController.php:50-62, wired at `routes/settings.php:16` `DELETE settings/profile`, no admin gate — any authenticated parent or tutor can call it today). `User` has no `SoftDeletes` (confirmed: no `deleted_at` in `database/migrations/0001_01_01_000000_create_users_table.php`, no trait on `app/Models/User.php`), so `$user->delete()` is a real, unguarded SQL `DELETE` on `main` right now. FK survey (`grep cascadeOnDelete|restrictOnDelete` across `database/migrations/*.php`): `tutor_profiles.user_id` and `learners.account_user_id` are both `cascadeOnDelete()`, while `lessons.tutor_profile_id` and `lessons.learner_id` are `restrictOnDelete()`. Net effect: a parent or tutor account with zero lessons ever is silently, unauditedly, permanently deleted along with its learners/tutor_profile/tutor_documents/availability rows (cascade) and any `match_requests` (also `cascadeOnDelete`) — no ledger/payment rows exist yet for such an account so no financial row is destroyed, but the deletion itself is unaudited and irreversible, contradicting R54 ("never hard-deleted... admin action, audited; not exposed to parents"). An account with any lesson history instead gets an uncaught `QueryException` (500) from the `restrictOnDelete` on `lessons`, since neither `ProfileController::destroy` nor `ProfileDeleteRequest` catches it. Severity: **Medium** (real invariant violation, live on `main`, exploitable by any signed-in parent/tutor today; no direct ledger/payment row is at risk because of the `restrictOnDelete` chain, which is what keeps this from being High) — logged here rather than silently patched, per rule 11's "production-affecting judgment call." In scope for this sub-cycle's own authorised work (R54's "deletion behaviour"), not a scope-widening ask: closing `settings/profile`'s DELETE route for parents/tutors and replacing it with the R54 admin-only anonymise path.

Design resolution, read directly from primary sources rather than guessed: the event's `(from, to)` pair has no distinct "skipped" status, so skip vs. cancel is decided by `from`, not `to` — confirmed via `app/Enums/LessonStatus.php` (no such case) and `app/Actions/Lessons/SkipLesson.php` (`$to` is always a cancelled-by-* value, guarded to only ever run `from Reserved`). Confirmed email fires on any `to === Confirmed`; cancelled fires on `from === Confirmed && to` cancelled-by-* (paid, `CancelLesson`); skipped fires on `from === Reserved && to` cancelled-by-* (free, `SkipLesson`). `CancelledPaymentFailed` confirmed out of CP3 scope by reading `docs/PLAN.md`/`docs/CHECKPOINTS.md` directly — that status belongs to the recurring-charge-failure flow, CP4/CP5. PRD line 157 ("Both") confirmed both parent (`$lesson->learner->account`) and tutor (`$lesson->tutorProfile->user`) are emailed for all three. Built: `SerializesModels` added to `LessonStatusChanged` (its first queued-listener consumers); three Mailables (`App\Mail\Lessons\{LessonConfirmedMail,LessonCancelledMail,LessonSkippedMail}`) using the existing `UsesSettingsSender` trait, matching `TutorApprovedMail`'s shape exactly; three Blade views (`resources/views/emails/lessons/{confirmed,cancelled,skipped}.blade.php`), each converting `starts_at` to the recipient's own `timezone` column per invariant #4; three auto-discovered listeners (`App\Listeners\Lessons\Send*`, `ShouldQueue`), no manual registration needed (confirmed via the existing Tutor-event listener pattern). WHERE reminder_24h_sent_at IS NULL`, a write that returns 1 row only for the instance that gets to queue the mail — not from `onOneServer`/`withoutOverlapping` alone, which protect the job but not the row." Full guidance, both slices:
Reminder job — atomic per-row claim (not scheduler-level locking) as the actual idempotency mechanism (`reminder_24h_sent_at`/`reminder_1h_sent_at`, already added at `117c9e5`); non-overlapping windows, 1h = `starts_at` in `(now, now+1h]`, 24h = `(now+1h, now+24h]`, to avoid a lesson double-firing both; `Confirmed`-status lessons only; the <24h-booking edge case decided by reading PRD first, else the simplest rule logged as a DECISION; command mirrors `ExpireUnpaidLessons`'s existing shape; test proves idempotency by literally running the job twice.
Dashboards — Inertia props are explicit arrays built by the controller, never serialized Eloquent models (invariant #8's contact-masking risk travels through eager-loaded relations otherwise); tutor "today" bounds computed in the tutor's own `timezone` then converted to UTC for the query; a new `LessonPolicy` with IDOR tests (403, status unchanged, nothing queued, for a lesson that isn't the actor's); cancel controller stays thin — Policy + Form Request, `CancelLesson` vs `SkipLesson` decided server-side from the lesson's own status, never a client-supplied field; tutor dashboard carries no cancel UI (parent-only per PLAN.md); Tailwind logical classes only, `npm run build` is a real gate for this slice, not a formality; browser sign-in to smoke-test is not available (would need a password, prohibited) — Inertia feature tests are the coverage, disclosed here as a limitation rather than silently skipped.

[2026-09-24 18:52] VERIFICATION — 24h/1h reminder job built per the `18:29` advisor guidance above. `SendLessonReminders` (`lessons:send-reminders`) mirrors `ExpireUnpaidLessons`'s shape: `lazyById()` sweep, `Confirmed`-only, half-open windows (1h = `starts_at` in `(now, now+1h]`, 24h = `(now+1h, now+24h]`) so a lesson never double-fires at the boundary and never needs special-casing for sub-24h bookings — it simply never enters the 24h window. Idempotency is the atomic per-row claim the advisor specified: `Lesson::where('id', $id)->whereNull($column)->update([$column => now()])`, only the run whose `UPDATE` affects exactly 1 row queues the mail; `onOneServer()->withoutOverlapping()` added to `routes/console.php` alongside it (stops a second worker starting; the claim is what makes one harmless if it starts anyway — both documented in the command's own docblock). `LessonReminderMail` is one Mailable for both windows (`'24h'|'1h'` literal-union `$window` param), matching `LessonConfirmedMail`/`LessonCancelledMail`'s shape exactly (`UsesSettingsSender`, queued); one shared Blade view, ternary on `$window` for "tomorrow"/"in about an hour" wording, `starts_at` converted to the recipient's own `timezone` per invariant #4. PHPStan initially flagged `sendWindow()`'s literal-union propagation (`$window` was plain `string`) — fixed with a `@param '1h'|'24h' $window` PHPDoc, re-ran 0 errors. 8 new tests in `SendLessonRemindersTest.php`: both windows fire correctly, >24h-away and already-started lessons fire neither, non-`Confirmed` lessons fire nothing, an already-sent row is not resent, running the job twice queues exactly 4 mails both times (not 8), and a render smoke test for both window strings.

[2026-09-24 18:52] DECISION — The <24h-booking edge case (a lesson confirmed with less than 24h until `starts_at`) gets no special-case code: the 24h window's own predicate, `starts_at` in `(now+1h, now+24h]`, is the entire rule — such a lesson is simply never inside that range, so it never receives a 24h reminder, only the 1h one (if it's still >1h away when confirmed) or neither (if under 1h). Reason: `docs/PRD.md:160` states only the two reminder triggers exist, nothing about a sub-24h grace case, and CLAUDE.md's "When unsure" default is the simplest option that keeps invariants intact — a bare window check is simpler and no less correct than an added branch. Confirmed by reading `docs/PRD.md` lines 145-169 directly, not inferred. Not a `DECISIONS.md`/ADR matter — implementation-scope call under an already-authorised PLAN.md step, logged here per the advisor's own framing of this as something to decide from the PRD or default rather than ask.

"You're about to build the dashboard slice, so this is the rule-11 design consult for `LessonPolicy`, the IDOR tests and the first HTTP cancel route. Log it as an ADVISOR entry immediately, quoting a line verbatim while it's still visible. Last time you reconstructed the quote from notes because the payload was encrypted, and you don't want that caveat twice.
Before code, two fixes on what you just pushed: STATUS.md header now says \"cycle 04 r9\"; it was r8. `rN` is the PLAN.md revision, and nothing this session shows a new PLAN revision. Check the PLAN.md header and `git log -- docs/PLAN.md`. If it's still r8, correct both places (header and §2) in the next STATUS write and say so in §6. This doesn't block code, but it must be fixed before PR #15, because a reviewer will catch a fabricated revision number. Rule 12 requires you to read back and diff every file you write. You committed STATUS.md and CYCLE-LOG without doing that, which is how the r9 slip got through. Run `git diff HEAD~1 -- docs/STATUS.md` on the next docs commit.
Design, in order: (1) Don't ask anyone about Reserved-lesson UX. The Explore agent suggested checking with the user, but rule 4 forbids asking and the 18:29 guidance already settled it. The server dispatches on status: Reserved → `SkipLesson`; Confirmed → `CancelLesson`; anything else (PendingPayment, terminal) → refuse with a toast, no action called. Keep this in a single three-arm `match` that tests can hit. Expose a server-computed `cancel_kind: 'skip'|'cancel'|null` prop so Vue only renders the button and label it's told to. Log this as a DECISION. (2) The cancel route goes in the parent group only (`can:access-parent-area`). `LessonPolicy::cancel` checks `Role::AccountOwner` and `learner->account_user_id === $user->id`. Add no tutor cancel route, even though `CancelLesson` accepts a tutor actor — that would widen scope. Catch `CancellationException` and flash a toast, as `LearnerController::destroy()` does; this covers the race where a Reserved lesson gets charged between the status read and the action's lock. (3) Converting `Route::inertia('dashboard', …)` to a controller: keep the route name `dashboard`. Grep tests for `component('Dashboard')` and for `RoleAccessTest`'s tutor-403 cases so the suite doesn't regress. `Dashboard.vue` imports `dashboard` from `@/routes`, which looks like generated route helpers (Wayfinder) — find out whether new routes need a regenerate step and whether the generated files are committed; that affects whether `npm run build` passes. (4) Tutor \"today\": check DATA_MODEL for which timezone column is authoritative, since availability uses tutor local time — check for a `tutor_profiles` timezone before defaulting to `users.timezone`. Make \"today\" and \"upcoming\" half-open and non-overlapping like the reminder windows: today = [local start of day, local end of day) converted to UTC; upcoming = on or after local end of day. The test must actually tell the cases apart — freeze the clock with `travelTo`, give the tutor a large-offset timezone, and place a lesson that falls on \"today\" in UTC but \"tomorrow\" locally. (5) IDOR tests: assert all of status unchanged, `Mail::assertNothingQueued()`, and ledger entry count unchanged — not just the 403. [2026-09-24 18:58] DECISION — Cancel-kind dispatch is a single three-arm `match` on the lesson's current status inside the new cancel controller: `Reserved` → `SkipLesson`, `Confirmed` → `CancelLesson`, anything else → refuse with a flashed toast and no Action call. The controller exposes this as a server-computed `cancel_kind: 'skip'|'cancel'|null` Inertia prop so the Vue button/label is server-decided, never inferred client-side. The cancel route is parent-only (`can:access-parent-area`); no tutor cancel route is added even though `CancelLesson` itself accepts a tutor actor, since PLAN.md's step 5 text names only the parent dashboard's cancel action. Per the `18:58` advisor consult; not a `DECISIONS.md`/ADR matter — implementation-scope call under the already-authorised step.

[2026-09-24 19:34] DECISION — Parent and tutor dashboard controllers built per the `18:58` advisor guidance. `TutorDashboardController::show()` reads `$user->tutorProfile` and returns `['today' => [], 'upcoming' => []]` with no query at all when it is `null`, rather than lazily creating a profile row from a GET request — a profile is only ever created from `TutorOnboardingController::profileFor()`, so a tutor who has never onboarded has certainly never had a bookable lesson. `DashboardController::show()`'s status set is `[Reserved, Confirmed]` (the two bookable-and-not-yet-happened states); `TutorDashboardController`'s "today" bucket additionally includes `InProgress` (a lesson already underway still belongs in "today", never silently drops off), while "upcoming" stays `[Reserved, Confirmed]` only. Tutor "today"/"upcoming" bucketing is a half-open, non-overlapping split on the tutor's own local day — `[local start of day, local end of day)` converted to UTC for "today", `>= local end of day` for "upcoming" — read from `users.timezone` (confirmed no `tutor_profiles`-level timezone column exists in `DATA_MODEL.md`, so `users.timezone` is authoritative, matching the reminder job's own precedent). Both controllers build explicit presenter arrays (`present()`) rather than passing Eloquent models to Inertia, carrying only display names (`learner->display_name`, `tutorProfile->displayName()`) and never `email`/`phone`/raw relations, per invariant #8 and the advisor's point (6); verified by `ParentDashboardTest.php`'s "never leaks a raw Eloquent model" test asserting the exact prop key set. Not a `DECISIONS.md`/ADR matter — implementation-scope calls under the already-authorised PLAN.md step, per the advisor's own framing.

[2026-09-24 19:34] NOTE — Two test-infrastructure findings from writing this slice's HTTP feature tests, worth recording so a future test-writer doesn't re-diagnose them: (1) `Inertia::flash('toast', [...])` does not store a literal `'toast'` session key — it stores everything under the package's own namespaced key (`SessionKey::FLASH_DATA`, `'inertia.flash_data'`), so `assertSessionHas('toast.type', ...)` always fails; the correct assertion is the package's own registered macro, `assertInertiaFlash('toast.type', $value)` (confirmed via `vendor/inertiajs/inertia-laravel/src/Testing/TestResponseMacros.php` and `AssertableInertia::assertFlashHas()`, which supports dot-notation via `Arr::has`/`Arr::get`). (2) `LearnerFactory`'s default `'curriculum_id' => Curriculum::factory()` is an unpinned, randomly-coded nested factory (not `firstOrCreate`); any test that creates more than one `Learner::factory()` without an explicit override has a real, empirically-reproduced chance of a `curricula_code_unique` violation when the random pick collides with an already-inserted code. Fixed the same way `LessonFactory` already documents for its own default curriculum: a `gcseCurriculumId()` helper doing `Curriculum::query()->firstOrCreate(['code' => CurriculumCode::Gcse], [...])`. First written duplicated per test file (Pest has no file namespacing, so identical top-level function names across files fatal-error with "Cannot redeclare" once more than one file loads in the same process) — that duplication itself then fatal-errored the moment `TutorDashboardTest.php`'s copy loaded alongside `ParentDashboardTest.php`'s in the same `php artisan test` run. Corrected by moving the single definition into `tests/Pest.php` (already the shared-global-helpers file, loaded exactly once for the whole `Feature` suite, per its existing `agreementReadyTutor()`/`ygRow()` helpers) and removing the three duplicates.

[2026-09-25 13:31] DEVIATION — R98's literal text for the lessons key is `unique (recurring_slot_id, starts_at) WHERE recurring_slot_id IS NOT NULL`. That contradicts R99 (pause cancels every future `reserved` lesson; resume "refills through generation from today"): the pause-cancelled rows would keep their keys and resume could not regenerate those weeks. 4a will instead create `lessons_recurring_slot_starts_at_unique ON lessons (recurring_slot_id, starts_at) WHERE recurring_slot_id IS NOT NULL AND cancel_reason IS DISTINCT FROM 'slot_paused'` (the value comes from a `LessonCancelReason::SlotPaused` enum constant, not a literal in call sites). A parent skip (`cancelled_by_parent`, any other reason) keeps its key, so the next `recurring:generate` cannot undo it. `lessons.cancel_reason` is `text NULL` (extend_lessons_table), so `IS DISTINCT FROM` is the null-safe form. This touches invariant 14's generation key, so it has its own advisor entry (above). Recorded in STATUS §6.

[2026-09-25 17:20] DECISION — Generation floors at today: an occurrence with `starts_at <= now` is dropped silently (no lesson, no skip), and the walk starts at `max(today, generated_until + 1, starts_on)`. [2026-09-25 17:20] DECISION — A recorded `recurring_slot_skips` row is final: the existing-set includes skip timestamps, so re-walking a date never turns a skip into a lesson (test: "never turns a recorded skip into a lesson…"). [2026-09-25 17:20] DECISION — A holding slot whose `effectiveEndDate()` (earlier of `ends_on`, `end_effective_on`) is before today in the slot's timezone is flipped to `ended` by the daily run, audited `recurring_slot.ended` with a null actor and reason "end date reached"; `ended_by_user_id`/`ended_at` set by 4b are not overwritten. [2026-09-25 18:05] DECISION — Permit hold (fix round 1, replaces the first design): while the tutor is `bookable()` now and an occurrence starts at or after the permit's UTC-midnight cutoff, generation stops the day before it, writes no skip and sends no email, and `generated_until` becomes `max(existing, that day)` — never backwards. [2026-09-25 18:10 machine clock] DECISION — Retry arithmetic (R101). Attempt 1 runs at `next_charge_at` (set at generation to `starts_at - recurring_charge_lead_hours`, 48h). After attempt n fails the next runs at `starts_at - recurring_retry_hours[n-1]` (36h, then 24h), never earlier than one hour from now (a late generated or late run lesson does not fire two attempts in the same hour), and if that time would not be before `starts_at` the failure is final. `charge_attempts` advances only when a result is recorded. Three attempts with the default settings; failure 3 cancels with `cancelled_payment_failed`, adds 1 to the slot's consecutive-failure counter, and at `recurring_pause_after_failures` (2) pauses the slot with reason `payment_failed`. Tests: T-48h/T-36h/T-24h ladder, late generation (T-30h) floored at now+1h, a lesson starting in 2h making attempt 2 final.

[2026-09-25 18:10 machine clock] DECISION — Auto-charge writes the HOLD inside the `reserved -> confirmed` transition closure through `LedgerService::hold()` (the same call `BookLesson` uses for a paid single booking), with the payment captured and `charge_attempts` set in the same transaction. No new ledger operation, no new state edge (R94). A failure to confirm after the money was taken is `NeedsReview` (payment captured, no hold, exception reported), which `ledger:verify` already flags after its five-minute grace; there is no refund path until CP5.

[2026-09-25 18:10 machine clock] DECISION — Missed-start sweep (the 4c carry). `recurring:charge` also selects reserved weekly lessons whose `starts_at` has passed, whether or not a gateway is bound, and cancels them `cancelled_payment_failed` with the new reason `charge_window_missed`. That is the platform's timing, not the parent's card, so it does not count against the slot's failure counter and cannot pause the slot. A `pending` attempt row left behind is not touched: it stays visible to `ledger:verify` as a stranded payment after the grace period. The parent and tutor get the cancelled email with wording that says the lesson could not be charged in time.

[2026-09-25 18:10 machine clock] DECISION — Emails (R102). New listener `SendWeeklyChargeMails` (queued, after commit, event-discovered): charged (parent only), charge failed with the retry time in the parent's timezone (parent only), cancelled for payment (parent and tutor). `SendLessonConfirmedMail` skips the parent's copy for `reserved -> confirmed`, because the weekly-charged email replaces it (the parent would otherwise get two); the tutor still gets the confirmed email. First version suppressed both and the full suite caught it (`LessonLifecycleMailTest` "emails both parent and tutor when a reserved lesson is confirmed" failed, 1304 of 1305); the tutor half was a regression, fixed in code, and that one existing test was rewritten to the new rule (tutor only, parent asserted not mailed) — the only existing test edited in 4e besides the three gateway doubles in `BookLessonTest`, to be named in the PR body.

Local seeded run through a bound fake driver: two weekly lessons at T-47h, one on a good card and one on the declining card — `recurring:charge` printed "Charged 1, retry scheduled 1, …, 0 lesson(s) errored"; lesson confirmed (attempt 1), lesson still reserved with `next_charge_at` 01:00 (the one-hour floor); `ledger:verify` OK afterwards (the local database now holds those two demo lessons). [2026-09-25 18:10 machine clock] DECISION — Parent resume on card replace (R101). `RecurringSlotPolicy::resume` now allows the parent of a slot paused with reason `payment_failed` (admin unchanged; a parent still cannot resume an admin pause). `ResumeRecurringSlot` requires a usable saved card whose `last_failed_at` is null — saving a card clears it — so pressing Resume with the card that declined is refused. The learner page shows the Resume button only when the policy allows it. Counter resets to 0 on resume, as for the admin.

[2026-09-25 18:10 machine clock] DECISION — `payments` uniqueness and key (R101, migration `2026_09_27_100000_restructure_payments_for_attempts`). Drops `unique(lesson_id)`; adds `attempt_no` (default 1), `unique(lesson_id, attempt_no)`, a partial unique index `payments_one_live_per_lesson` on `(lesson_id) WHERE status <> 'failed'` (a lesson has failed attempts and at most one live one, enforced by the database), and an FK `payment_method_id -> payment_methods` (restrict). Gateway idempotency key `lesson:{id}:attempt:{n}`. Forward-only; DATA_MODEL v1.5 records it. Rehearsal has no `payments` rows to convert (no booking path outside tests until CP5).

[2026-09-26 01:05 machine clock] DECISION — Pending-attempt exception in the R104 branch (CC, in the fix loop, advisor-driven). The owner's R104 says "never charged". A lesson with a Pending attempt already began charging; cancelling over it risks a captured payment with no ledger entry (invariant 1 in practice), which is worse than finishing the attempt. So while a Pending attempt exists the branch does not fire and the attempt is finished with the same idempotency key. Disclosed as a narrow departure from the letter of R104; it is reached only after a crash between `begin()` and the gateway call. The reviewer's finding 1 below argues the other side of this trade-off. (cycle 05 r2)
